# Gene paragraph drafts

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

All 1,305 entries remain drafts. Includes the original 1,133-gene roster and 172 hotspot supplement genes. See EVIDENCE-REVIEW.md and HOTSPOT-REVIEW.md for limitations.

## ABCB11

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ABCB11 encodes bile salt export pump. It participates in cholesterol homeostasis and lipid homeostasis. Functional annotations also associate the protein with ATP hydrolysis activity and ABC-type xenobiotic transporter activity. ([NCBI Gene 8647](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/8647), [UniProt O95342](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/O95342/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with progressive familial intrahepatic cholestasis type 2 (autosomal recessive). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)).

Curation note (outside report text): somatic_association_requires_curation.

## ABCC10

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ABCC10 encodes ATP binding cassette subfamily C member 10. Its functions include ABC-type glutathione S-conjugate transporter activity and ABC-type transporter activity. ([NCBI Gene 89845](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/89845), [UniProt Q5T3U5](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q5T3U5/entry)). Somatic alterations involving ABCC10 have been reported in glioblastoma, breast carcinoma, and low-grade glioma. ([PMID 26619011](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26619011/)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## ABL1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ABL1 encodes tyrosine-protein kinase ABL1. It participates in mismatch repair and intracellular signal transduction. Functional annotations also associate the protein with kinase activity and protein kinase activity. ([NCBI Gene 25](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/25), [UniProt P00519](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P00519/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with congenital heart defects and skeletal malformations syndrome (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving ABL1 have been reported in acute myeloid leukemia, chronic myeloid leukemia, and b-lymphoblastic leukemia/lymphoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

## ABL2

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ABL2 encodes tyrosine-protein kinase ABL2. It participates in signal transduction and negative regulation of Rho protein signal transduction. Functional annotations also associate the protein with protein kinase activity and protein tyrosine kinase activity. ([NCBI Gene 27](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/27), [UniProt P42684](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P42684/entry)). ABL2 can participate in leukemia-associated rearrangements, including fusion with ETV6. ([NCBI Gene 27](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/27)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## ACADS

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ACADS encodes acyl-CoA dehydrogenase short chain. Its functions include acyl-CoA dehydrogenase activity and flavin adenine dinucleotide binding. ([NCBI Gene 35](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/35), [UniProt P16219](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P16219/entry)). Somatic alterations involving ACADS have been reported in glioblastoma, low-grade glioma, and colorectal carcinoma. ([PMID 26619011](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26619011/)).

## ACTB

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ACTB encodes actin, cytoplasmic 1 [Cleaved into: Actin, cytoplasmic 1, N-terminally processed]. It participates in positive regulation of DNA-templated transcription and regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Functional annotations also associate the protein with ATP hydrolysis activity and transporter regulator activity. ([NCBI Gene 60](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/60), [UniProt P60709](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P60709/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with Baraitser-Winter cerebrofrontofacial syndrome (autosomal dominant); Baraitser-Winter syndrome 1 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 22366783](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22366783/), [PMID 23649928](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23649928/)). Somatic alterations involving ACTB have been reported in GLI1-altered mesenchymal tumor, malignant/metastasizing. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

## ACVR1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ACVR1 encodes activin receptor type-1. It participates in negative regulation of signal transduction and positive regulation of DNA-templated transcription. Functional annotations also associate the protein with protein kinase activity and protein serine/threonine kinase activity. ([NCBI Gene 90](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/90), [UniProt Q04771](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q04771/entry)). ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## AFF2

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

## APC

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

APC encodes a tumor suppressor that restrains Wnt signaling and also participates in cell adhesion, migration, and regulation of gene expression. These functions connect control of cell growth with interactions between neighboring cells. In colorectal tumors, disease-associated APC sequence changes commonly produce a shortened protein and cluster within a particular part of the gene, termed the mutation cluster region. ([NCBI Gene 324](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/324)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with classic or attenuated familial adenomatous polyposis (autosomal dominant); gastric adenocarcinoma and proximal polyposis of the stomach (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving APC have been reported in colorectal adenocarcinoma, small intestinal adenocarcinoma, and gastric adenocarcinoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

APLNR encodes apelin receptor. It participates in vasculogenesis and aorta development. Functional annotations also associate the protein with mechanoreceptor activity and apelin receptor activity. ([NCBI Gene 187](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/187), [UniProt P35414](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P35414/entry)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation, somatic_association_requires_curation.

## APOBR

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

APOBR encodes apolipoprotein B receptor. Its functions include lipoprotein particle receptor activity and very-low-density lipoprotein particle receptor activity. ([NCBI Gene 55911](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/55911), [UniProt Q0VD83](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q0VD83/entry)). Somatic alterations involving APOBR have been reported in breast carcinoma, ovarian carcinoma, and cervical carcinoma. ([PMID 26619011](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26619011/)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## AR

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

AR encodes androgen receptor. It participates in signal transduction and positive regulation of DNA-templated transcription. Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA-binding transcription factor activity and DNA-binding transcription activator activity, RNA polymerase II-specific. ([NCBI Gene 367](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/367), [UniProt P10275](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P10275/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with Kennedy disease (x-linked); androgen insensitivity syndrome (x-linked). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving AR have been reported in prostate adenocarcinoma, prostate, and cholangiocarcinoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [PMID 29610475](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29610475/), [PMID 31061129](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31061129/)).

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ARAF encodes serine/threonine-protein kinase A-Raf. It participates in MAPK cascade and protein modification process. Functional annotations also associate the protein with protein kinase activity and protein serine kinase activity. ## ARID1A

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ARID1A encodes a DNA-binding component of the SWI/SNF chromatin-remodeling complex. This complex uses energy from ATP to change the organization of chromatin, allowing access to genes that would otherwise be repressed. ARID1A helps direct the complex to its targets through interactions with DNA or other proteins; it is a targeting component rather than the ATPase that powers remodeling. ([NCBI Gene 8289](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/8289), [UniProt O14497](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/O14497/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with Coffin-Siris syndrome (autosomal dominant); intellectual disability, autosomal dominant 14 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving ARID1A have been reported in urothelial carcinoma, gastric adenocarcinoma, and hepatocellular carcinoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## ARID1B

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ARID1B encodes AT-rich interactive domain-containing protein 1B. It participates in transcription initiation-coupled chromatin remodeling and positive regulation of DNA-templated transcription. Functional annotations also associate the protein with transcription coactivator activity. ([NCBI Gene 57492](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/57492), [UniProt Q8NFD5](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q8NFD5/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with Coffin-Siris syndrome (autosomal dominant); Coffin-Siris syndrome 1 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving ARID1B have been reported in undifferentiated / dedifferentiated endometrial carcinoma, biphasic hyalinizing psammomatous / NF2-inactivated renal cell carcinoma, and angiosarcoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ARID2 encodes AT-rich interactive domain-containing protein 2. It participates in regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II and positive regulation of double-strand break repair via homologous recombination. Functional annotations also associate the protein with ATP-dependent chromatin remodeler activity. ([NCBI Gene 196528](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/196528), [UniProt Q68CP9](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q68CP9/entry)). ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

## ASXL2

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ASXL2 encodes putative Polycomb group protein ASXL2. It participates in positive regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II and positive regulation of fat cell differentiation. Functional annotations also associate the protein with chromatin binding and peroxisome proliferator activated receptor binding. ([NCBI Gene 55252](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/55252), [UniProt Q76L83](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q76L83/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with syndromic intellectual disability (autosomal dominant); Shashi-Pena syndrome (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic ASXL2 mutations have been reported in several cancer types, including breast, bladder, pancreatic, ovarian, prostate, and hematologic tumors. ([NCBI Gene 55252](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/55252)).

## BRAF

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

BRAF encodes a serine/threonine kinase in the RAF family that relays growth signals through the MAPK/ERK pathway. This signaling pathway helps regulate cell division, differentiation, and secretion. Activating changes in BRAF can increase this signaling output; V600E is a well-characterized example, but the gene's normal role is to transmit regulated signals rather than maintain continuous pathway activation. ([NCBI Gene 673](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/673)). Germline disease associations include Cardiofaciocutaneous syndrome (autosomal dominant). ([GeneReviews: Cardiofaciocutaneous Syndrome](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1186/), [PMID 16474404](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16474404/)). Somatic activating BRAF mutations occur in melanoma, colorectal and thyroid carcinomas, lung carcinoma, and hairy cell leukemia, among other neoplasms. ([NCBI Gene 673](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/673), [PMID 22817889](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22817889/)).

## BRCA1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

BRCA1 encodes a nuclear tumor suppressor involved in maintaining genome stability. It works with DNA-damage sensors, other tumor suppressors, and signaling proteins in multiprotein assemblies that coordinate responses to damaged DNA. Its functions include repair of DNA double-strand breaks, recombination, and regulation of transcription. Interactions with RNA polymerase II and chromatin-associated complexes link these DNA-repair functions with regulation of gene activity. ([NCBI Gene 672](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/672)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with BRCA1-related cancer predisposition (autosomal dominant); Fanconi anemia, complementation group S (autosomal recessive). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 25472942](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25472942/), [PMID 23269703](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23269703/)). Somatic alterations involving BRCA1 have been reported in high-grade serous carcinoma, tubo-ovarian / peritoneal, invasive breast carcinoma, and pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [PMID 24240112](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24240112/), [PMID 21720365](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21720365/), [PMID 25719666](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25719666/)).

## BRCA2

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

BRCA2 encodes a tumor suppressor that maintains genome stability through homologous recombination and protection of DNA replication forks. The protein binds DNA and interacts with RAD51, helping assemble RAD51 filaments at DNA double-strand breaks. It also interacts with PALB2. These coordinated activities support accurate repair during cell division and help protect DNA while it is being copied. ([NCBI Gene 675](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/675)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with BRCA2-related cancer predisposition (autosomal dominant); Fanconi anemia complementation group D1 (autosomal recessive). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving BRCA2 have been reported in invasive breast carcinoma, high-grade serous carcinoma, tubo-ovarian / peritoneal, and prostate adenocarcinoma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [PMID 29610475](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29610475/), [PMID 24240112](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24240112/)).

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

BRIP1 encodes Fanconi anemia group J protein. It participates in DNA repair and homologous recombination. ## BTK

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

BTK encodes Bruton tyrosine kinase, a signaling protein important for B-cell development and transmission of B-cell antigen-receptor signals. ([NCBI Gene 695](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/695)). Germline disease associations include Bruton-type agammaglobulinemia (x-linked). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Acquired BTK mutations are documented in chronic lymphocytic leukemia under BTK-inhibitor selective pressure. This treatment-associated somatic context is distinct from constitutional BTK loss of function causing immunodeficiency. ([PMID 24869598](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24869598/)).

Somatic alterations involving CD79B have been reported in diffuse large b-cell lymphoma, intravascular large b-cell lymphoma, and large b-cell lymphoma of immune-privileged sites. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

## CTNNB1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

CTNNB1 encodes beta-catenin, a component of adherens junctions that connect neighboring epithelial cells. Beta-catenin links these junctions with the actin cytoskeleton and participates in signaling that couples cell contact with control of proliferation. It also interacts with the APC tumor suppressor. Its roles in cell adhesion and growth regulation connect the organization of epithelial tissues with control of cell division. ([NCBI Gene 1499](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/1499)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with CTNNB1-related neurodevelopmental disorder and/or vitreoretinopathy (autosomal dominant); severe intellectual disability-progressive spastic diplegia syndrome (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving CTNNB1 have been reported in hepatocellular carcinoma, combined hepatocellular-cholangiocarcinoma, and endometrioid carcinoma of endometrium. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## CUL3

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

## DGCR8

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

DGCR8 encodes the non-catalytic RNA-binding component of the microprocessor complex, which carries out an early step in microRNA production. This division of labor separates RNA recognition from RNA cutting: DGCR8 binds the substrate, while DROSHA supplies the ribonuclease activity. ([NCBI Gene 54487](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/54487), [UniProt Q8WYQ5](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q8WYQ5/entry)). Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA-methyltransferase activity and DNA (cytosine-5-)-methyltransferase activity. ([NCBI Gene 1786](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/1786), [UniProt P26358](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P26358/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with autosomal dominant cerebellar ataxia, deafness and narcolepsy (autosomal dominant); hereditary sensory neuropathy-deafness-dementia syndrome (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 21532572](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21532572/)).

Curation note (outside report text): somatic_association_requires_curation.

## DNMT3A

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

DNMT3A encodes a DNA methyltransferase involved in establishing new DNA-methylation patterns, rather than simply copying an existing pattern after DNA replication. Methylation of CpG sites is an epigenetic modification important in development, genomic imprinting, and X-chromosome inactivation. DNMT3A expression is developmentally regulated, linking its role in DNA modification to changes in cellular state during development. ([NCBI Gene 1788](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/1788)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with Tatton-Brown-Rahman overgrowth syndrome (autosomal dominant); Heyn-Sproul-Jackson syndrome (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 24614070](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24614070/), [PMID 29900417](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29900417/), [PMID 30478443](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30478443/)). Somatic alterations involving DNMT3A have been reported in acute myeloid leukemia, myelodysplastic syndromes, and peripheral t-cell lymphoma, NOS. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [PMID 30333627](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30333627/)).

## DROSHA

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

DROSHA encodes the catalytic ribonuclease III component of the microprocessor complex. In the nucleus, it cleaves primary microRNA transcripts to release stem-loop precursor microRNAs. These precursors are subsequently exported to the cytoplasm for further processing. DROSHA therefore acts early in the microRNA-production pathway, working with an RNA-binding partner rather than carrying out all of the steps needed to produce mature microRNAs. ([NCBI Gene 29102](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/29102), [UniProt Q9NRR4](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q9NRR4/entry)). Somatic alterations involving DROSHA have been reported in Wilms tumor, pineoblastoma, and wilms' tumor. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## EGFR

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

EGFR encodes a cell-surface receptor tyrosine kinase that recognizes members of the epidermal growth factor family. Ligand binding promotes receptor dimerization and phosphorylation of tyrosine residues on the receptor itself, initiating signals that support cell proliferation. Its normal activity thus couples an extracellular growth signal to an intracellular response through regulated receptor assembly and kinase activation. ([NCBI Gene 1956](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/1956)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with non-small cell lung carcinoma (autosomal dominant); lung cancer (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving EGFR have been reported in lung adenocarcinoma, glioblastoma, IDH-wildtype, and glioblastoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [PMID 30150660](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30150660/)).

([NCBI Gene 2034](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/2034), [UniProt Q99814](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q99814/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with erythrocytosis, familial, 4 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving EPAS1 have been reported in paraganglioma and pheochromocytoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

EPC1 encodes enhancer of polycomb homolog 1. It participates in DNA-templated transcription and chromatin organization. Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

FAAP24 encodes Fanconi anemia core complex-associated protein 24. It participates in interstrand cross-link repair. Functional annotations also associate the protein with chromatin binding. ([NCBI Gene 91442](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/91442), [UniProt Q9BTP7](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q9BTP7/entry)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation, somatic_association_requires_curation.

## FANK1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

FANK1 encodes fibronectin type III and ankyrin repeat domains 1. Its functions include negative regulation of apoptotic process and positive regulation of apoptotic process. ## FAS

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

FAS encodes tumor necrosis factor receptor superfamily member 6. It participates in signal transduction and apoptotic process. Functional annotations also associate the protein with signaling receptor activity and tumor necrosis factor receptor activity. ([NCBI Gene 355](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/355), [UniProt P25445](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P25445/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with FAS-related autoimmune lymphoproliferative immune disorder (semidominant); autoimmune lymphoproliferative syndrome type 1 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 21490157](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21490157/)). Somatic alterations involving FAS have been reported in malignant lymphoma, cervical squamous cell carcinoma, and diffuse large b-Cell lymphoma, NOS. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [PMID 29713087](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29713087/)).

## FAT1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

FAT1 encodes protocadherin Fat 1 [Cleaved into: Protocadherin Fat 1, nuclear form]. It participates in cell adhesion and cell migration. Functional annotations also associate the protein with calcium ion binding. ([NCBI Gene 2195](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/2195), [UniProt Q14517](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q14517/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with focal segmental glomerulosclerosis (autosomal recessive). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving FAT1 have been reported in pleomorphic dermal sarcoma, metaplastic breast carcinoma, and biphasic hyalinizing psammomatous / NF2-inactivated renal cell carcinoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

## FBXO11

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

FBXO11 encodes f-box only protein 11. It participates in protein ubiquitination and sensory perception of sound. Functional annotations also associate the protein with protein-arginine N-methyltransferase activity and ubiquitin-protein transferase activity. ([NCBI Gene 80204](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/80204), [UniProt Q86XK2](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q86XK2/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with intellectual developmental disorder with dysmorphic facies and behavioral abnormalities (autosomal dominant). ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## FBXW7

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

FBXW7 encodes f-box/WD repeat-containing protein 7. It participates in lipid homeostasis and protein stabilization. Functional annotations also associate the protein with protein-macromolecule adaptor activity and ubiquitin-like ligase-substrate adaptor activity. ([NCBI Gene 55294](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/55294), [UniProt Q969H0](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q969H0/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with developmental delay, hypotonia, and impaired language (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## FGFR1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

FGFR1 encodes fibroblast growth factor receptor 1. It participates in positive regulation of MAP kinase activity and positive regulation of phosphatidylinositol 3-kinase/protein kinase B signal transduction. Functional annotations also associate the protein with protein tyrosine kinase activity. ([NCBI Gene 2260](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/2260), [UniProt P11362](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P11362/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with Pfeiffer syndrome type 1 (autosomal dominant); Hartsfield-Bixler-Demyer syndrome (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 23812909](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23812909/)). Postzygotic mosaic variants are associated with encephalocraniocutaneous lipomatosis. ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving FGFR1 have been reported in lung squamous cell carcinoma, phosphaturic mesenchymal tumor, malignant, and high-grade astrocytoma with piloid features. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

## FGFR2

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

FGFR2 encodes fibroblast growth factor receptor 2. It participates in negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II and positive regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

## FLI1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

FLI1 encodes friend leukemia integration 1 transcription factor. It participates in positive regulation of DNA-templated transcription and regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

([NCBI Gene 8328](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/8328), [UniProt Q5VTD9](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q5VTD9/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with platelet-type bleeding disorder 17 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving GFI1B have been reported in medulloblastoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

GID4 encodes glucose-induced degradation protein 4 homolog. It participates in proteasome-mediated ubiquitin-dependent protein catabolic process. ([NCBI Gene 79018](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/79018), [UniProt Q8IVV7](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q8IVV7/entry)).

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

GLI1 encodes transcription activator GLI1. It participates in regulation of DNA-templated transcription and positive regulation of DNA-templated transcription. ## HRAS

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

HRAS encodes a small GTPase that binds GDP or GTP and participates in intracellular signal transmission. The protein also moves between the Golgi apparatus and the plasma membrane. Reversible attachment and removal of palmitate regulate this trafficking, linking its cellular location with its signaling role. These properties provide both a nucleotide-dependent signaling cycle and a mechanism for controlling where the protein acts. ([NCBI Gene 3265](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/3265)). Germline disease associations include Costello syndrome (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving HRAS have been reported in head and neck squamous cell carcinoma, bladder urothelial carcinoma, and salivary duct carcinoma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [PMID 24121792](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24121792/)).

Pathogenic germline variants are associated with cerebral arteriopathy, autosomal recessive, with subcortical infarcts and leukoencephalopathy 2 (autosomal recessive); cerebral arteriopathy, autosomal dominant, with subcortical infarcts and leukoencephalopathy, type 2 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)).

Curation note (outside report text): somatic_association_requires_curation.

## IDH1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

IDH1 encodes an NADP-dependent isocitrate dehydrogenase located mainly in the cytoplasm and peroxisomes. The enzyme converts isocitrate to alpha-ketoglutarate, also called 2-oxoglutarate, while generating NADPH. Cytoplasmic NADPH production and regeneration of NADPH within peroxisomes are important aspects of its normal function. Its subcellular location distinguishes IDH1 from the related mitochondrial NADP-dependent enzyme encoded by IDH2. ([NCBI Gene 3417](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/3417/)). Postzygotic mosaic variants are associated with Ollier disease and Maffucci syndrome. ([IDH1 gene — MedlinePlus Genetics](https://medlineplus.gov/genetics/gene/idh1/)). Somatic IDH1 mutations occur in diffuse gliomas, acute myeloid leukemia, and cholangiocarcinoma. Cancer-associated R132 variants can acquire a neomorphic activity that produces D-2-hydroxyglutarate. ([Association of TP53 Alteration with Tissue Specificity and Patient Outcome of IDH1-Mutant Glioma](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8394030/), [PMID 19935646](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19935646/), [PMID 30333627](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30333627/)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## IDH2

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

IDH2 encodes a mitochondrial NADP-dependent isocitrate dehydrogenase. It catalyzes conversion of isocitrate to alpha-ketoglutarate, also called 2-oxoglutarate, and contributes to intermediary metabolism and energy production. Unlike the predominantly cytoplasmic and peroxisomal enzyme encoded by IDH1, IDH2 functions within mitochondria. The normal enzyme belongs to the NADP-dependent subgroup of isocitrate dehydrogenases and operates as a homodimer. ([NCBI Gene 3418](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/3418)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with mitochondrial disease (autosomal dominant); d-2-hydroxyglutaric aciduria 2 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving IDH2 have been reported in acute myeloid leukemia, sinonasal undifferentiated carcinoma, and chondrosarcoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [PMID 30333627](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30333627/)).

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

## KMT2C

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

KMT2C encodes histone-lysine N-methyltransferase 2C. It participates in positive regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Functional annotations also associate the protein with histone methyltransferase activity and transcription coactivator activity. ([NCBI Gene 58508](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/58508), [UniProt Q8NEZ4](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q8NEZ4/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with syndromic intellectual disability (autosomal dominant); Kleefstra syndrome 2 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving KMT2C have been reported in melanoma, pancreas, and prostate. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## KMT2D

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

KMT2D encodes histone-lysine N-methyltransferase 2D. It participates in regulation of DNA-templated transcription and positive regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Functional annotations also associate the protein with transcription coactivator activity and histone H3K4 methyltransferase activity. ([NCBI Gene 8085](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/8085), [UniProt O14686](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/O14686/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with Kabuki syndrome 1 (autosomal dominant); choanal atresia-athelia-hypothyroidism-delayed puberty-short stature syndrome (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving KMT2D have been reported in follicular lymphoma, marginal zone lymphoma, and adamantinoma of long bones. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

## KMT5A

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

KMT5A encodes n-lysine methyltransferase KMT5A. It participates in negative regulation of DNA-templated transcription and negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Functional annotations also associate the protein with histone methyltransferase activity and lysine N-methyltransferase activity. ([NCBI Gene 387893](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/387893), [UniProt Q9NQR1](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q9NQR1/entry)). Somatic alterations involving KMT5A have been reported in invasive breast carcinoma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## KNSTRN

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

KNSTRN encodes small kinetochore-associated protein. It participates in cell migration and chromosome segregation. Functional annotations also associate the protein with microtubule plus-end binding. Somatic alterations involving KNSTRN have been reported in cutaneous melanoma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## KRAS

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

KRAS encodes a small GTPase that switches between GDP-bound and GTP-bound states to relay signals controlling cell proliferation and survival. Activating amino-acid substitutions can disrupt the normal regulation of this signaling function. Alternative splicing produces two protein isoforms with different carboxy-terminal regions, so the gene gives rise to distinct protein forms while retaining its central role in intracellular signal transmission. ([NCBI Gene 3845](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/3845)). Germline disease associations include Noonan syndrome (autosomal dominant); cardiofaciocutaneous syndrome (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 16474405](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16474405/), [PMID 16474404](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16474404/)). Somatic alterations involving KRAS have been reported in pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma, colorectal adenocarcinoma, and lung adenocarcinoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [PMID 26619011](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26619011/), [PMID 29247016](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29247016/)).

Somatic alterations involving MALT1 have been reported in marginal zone lymphoma and diffuse large b-Cell lymphoma, NOS. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## MLH1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

MLH1 promoter methylation is a distinct mechanism of tumor-associated silencing. ## NF1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

NF1 encodes neurofibromin, a negative regulator of RAS signal transmission. Its normal role helps restrain signaling through a pathway involved in cell growth and proliferation. The gene produces alternatively spliced transcripts encoding different protein isoforms. Its messenger RNA can also undergo editing that creates a premature termination signal, providing an additional level of regulation of the encoded protein product. ([NCBI Gene 4763](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/4763)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with neurofibromatosis type 1 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving NF1 have been reported in malignant peripheral nerve sheath tumor, glioblastoma, IDH-wildtype, and glioblastoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## NF2

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

NF2 encodes merlin, a protein related to the ezrin-radixin-moesin family that connects cell-membrane proteins with the cytoskeleton. Merlin participates in cell adhesion, signaling across the membrane, and contact-dependent inhibition of proliferation. It interacts with cell-surface proteins and regulators of cytoskeletal dynamics, helping link physical contacts between cells to signals that limit further cell division. ([NCBI Gene 4771](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/4771)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with NF2-related schwannomatosis (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving NF2 have been reported in meningioma, CNS WHO grade 3, mesothelioma, and pleural mesothelioma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

([NCBI Gene 4794](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/4794), [UniProt O00221](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/O00221/entry)). Somatic alterations involving NFKBIE have been reported in malignant lymphoma, lung adenocarcinoma, and non-Hodgkin lymphoma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## NPM1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

NPM1 encodes nucleophosmin, a phosphoprotein that moves between the nucleolus, nucleus, and cytoplasm. It acts as a protein chaperone for ribosomal proteins and core histones and participates in centrosome duplication and cell proliferation. Nucleophosmin also retains the ARF tumor suppressor in the nucleolus, where it can be protected from degradation, connecting intracellular protein handling with regulation of growth-control pathways. ([NCBI Gene 4869](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/4869)). Rare germline NPM1 variants have been reported in dyskeratosis congenita; evidence for a broader inherited bone-marrow-failure syndrome is limited. ([PMID 31570891](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31570891/), [ClinGen SGC-107495.2](https://search.clinicalgenome.org/kb/gene-validity/CGGV:assertion_53473fb7-51bd-4ce9-b447-9113dfa8bb70-2025-04-08T16:00:00.000Z)). Somatic alterations involving NPM1 have been reported in acute myeloid leukemia, myeloid sarcoma, and anaplastic large cell lymphoma, systemic. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

## NRAS

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

NRAS encodes a small GTPase whose activity is controlled by proteins that promote nucleotide exchange or stimulate GTP hydrolysis. NRAS also cycles between the Golgi apparatus and the plasma membrane. Reversible palmitoylation, regulated by the ZDHHC9-GOLGA7 complex, helps control this trafficking. Regulation of both nucleotide binding and cellular location therefore contributes to the normal control of NRAS signaling. ([NCBI Gene 4893](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/4893)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with Noonan syndrome (autosomal dominant); Noonan syndrome 6 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 19966803](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19966803/), [PMID 21263000](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21263000/)). Somatic alterations involving NRAS have been reported in mucosal melanoma, cutaneous melanoma, and conjunctival melanoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [PMID 22817889](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22817889/)).

## PIK3CA

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

PIK3CA encodes the p110-alpha catalytic subunit of phosphatidylinositol 3-kinase. The enzyme combines a catalytic subunit with an approximately 85-kDa regulatory subunit and uses ATP to phosphorylate phosphoinositide lipids. Its normal role is therefore to generate lipid signaling products through regulated enzymatic activity. The distinction between catalytic and regulatory components is central to understanding its place in the PI3K signaling pathway. ([NCBI Gene 5290](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/5290)). Germline disease associations include overgrowth syndrome and/or cerebral malformations due to abnormalities in MTOR pathway genes (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Postzygotic mosaic variants are associated with megalencephaly-capillary malformation-polymicrogyria syndrome. ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 22729224](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22729224/)). Somatic alterations involving PIK3CA have been reported in invasive breast carcinoma of no special type, endometrioid carcinoma of endometrium, and colorectal adenocarcinoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## PMS2

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

PMS2 encodes the endonuclease component of the MLH1–PMS2 mismatch-repair complex, which helps remove replication-associated DNA errors. ([NCBI Gene 5395](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/5395)). Germline disease associations include Lynch syndrome (autosomal dominant); Constitutional mismatch repair deficiency (autosomal recessive). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Loss of mismatch-repair function involving PMS2 is relevant in colorectal and endometrial carcinomas. ([AMP sporadic solid tumors — MMR deficiency](https://www.amp.org/AMP/assets/File/education/MIMP/SolidTumors_Sporadic.pdf)).

## PNKP

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

PNKP encodes bifunctional polynucleotide phosphatase/kinase [Includes: Polynucleotide 3'-phosphatase (2'(3')-polynucleotidase); Polynucleotide 5'-hydroxyl-kinase]. It participates in DNA repair and regulation of DNA repair. Functional annotations also associate the protein with ATP-dependent polydeoxyribonucleotide 5'-hydroxyl-kinase activity and endonuclease activity. ([NCBI Gene 11284](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/11284), [UniProt Q96T60](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q96T60/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with microcephaly, seizures, and developmental delay (autosomal recessive); ataxia - oculomotor apraxia type 4 (autosomal recessive). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 20118933](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20118933/), [PMID 25728773](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25728773/)).

Curation note (outside report text): somatic_association_requires_curation.

## PNLIPRP3

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

PNLIPRP3 encodes pancreatic lipase related protein 3. Its functions include glycerophospholipid phospholipase A1 activity and lipoprotein lipase activity. ([NCBI Gene 119548](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/119548), [UniProt Q17RR3](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q17RR3/entry)). Somatic alterations involving PNLIPRP3 have been reported in esophageal carcinoma and gastric adenocarcinoma. ([PMID 26619011](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26619011/)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## PNRC1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

PNRC1 encodes proline-rich nuclear receptor coactivator 1. It participates in nuclear-transcribed mRNA catabolic process. ([NCBI Gene 10957](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/10957), [UniProt Q12796](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q12796/entry)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation, somatic_association_requires_curation.

## PODXL

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

PODXL encodes podocalyxin like. Its functions include cell migration and epithelial tube formation. ([NCBI Gene 5420](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/5420), [UniProt O00592](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/O00592/entry)). Somatic alterations involving PODXL have been reported in adrenocortical carcinoma, papillary renal cell carcinoma, and clear cell renal cell carcinoma. ([PMID 26619011](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26619011/)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## POLB

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

POLB encodes DNA polymerase beta. It participates in DNA repair and regulation of DNA repair. Functional annotations also associate the protein with lyase activity and 5'-deoxyribose-5-phosphate lyase activity. ([NCBI Gene 5423](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/5423), [UniProt P06746](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P06746/entry)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation, somatic_association_requires_curation.

## POLD1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

POLD1 encodes the catalytic subunit of DNA polymerase delta, which participates in DNA replication and repair and provides proofreading exonuclease activity. ([NCBI Gene 5424](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/5424)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with POLD1-related polyposis and colorectal cancer syndrome (autosomal dominant); mandibular hypoplasia-deafness-progeroid syndrome (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 23263490](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23263490/), [PMID 23770608](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23770608/)). Somatic alterations involving POLD1 have been reported in esophageal adenocarcinoma and invasive breast carcinoma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## POLE

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

POLE encodes the catalytic subunit of DNA polymerase epsilon, which participates in DNA replication and contains a proofreading exonuclease domain. ([NCBI Gene 5426](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/5426)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with POLE-related polyposis and colorectal cancer syndrome (autosomal dominant); colorectal cancer, susceptibility to, 12 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 23263490](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23263490/)). Somatic alterations involving POLE have been reported in colorectal adenocarcinoma and endometrioid carcinoma of endometrium. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

## POLH

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

POLH encodes DNA polymerase eta. It participates in DNA repair and regulation of DNA repair. Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA polymerase activity and DNA-directed DNA polymerase activity. ([NCBI Gene 5429](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/5429), [UniProt Q9Y253](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q9Y253/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with xeroderma pigmentosum variant type (autosomal recessive). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)).

Curation note (outside report text): somatic_association_requires_curation.

## POLQ

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

POLQ encodes DNA polymerase theta [Includes: Helicase POLQ; DNA polymerase POLQ]. It participates in DNA repair and negative regulation of double-strand break repair via homologous recombination. Functional annotations also associate the protein with 5'-deoxyribose-5-phosphate lyase activity and ATP hydrolysis activity. ([NCBI Gene 10721](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/10721), [UniProt O75417](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/O75417/entry)). Somatic alterations involving POLQ have been reported in melanoma, endometrial carcinoma, and lung squamous cell carcinoma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

POM121 encodes POM121 transmembrane nucleoporin. Its functions include structural constituent of nuclear pore and nucleocytoplasmic transport. Somatic alterations involving POM121 have been reported in glioblastoma, cutaneous melanoma, and prostate adenocarcinoma. ([PMID 26619011](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26619011/)).

## POT1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

POT1 encodes protection of telomeres protein 1. It participates in telomere maintenance via telomerase and positive regulation of telomere maintenance. Functional annotations also associate the protein with telomerase inhibitor activity and 8-hydroxy-2'-deoxyguanosine DNA binding. ([NCBI Gene 25913](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/25913), [UniProt Q9NUX5](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q9NUX5/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with tumor predisposition syndrome 3 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 24686849](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24686849/)). Somatic alterations involving POT1 have been reported in angiosarcoma and chronic lymphocytic leukemia/Small lymphocytic lymphoma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [PMID 23415222](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23415222/)).

## POU5F1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

POU5F1 encodes POU domain, class 5, transcription factor 1. It participates in regulation of DNA-templated transcription and negative regulation of miRNA transcription. Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA-binding transcription factor activity and DNA-binding transcription factor activity, RNA polymerase II-specific. ([NCBI Gene 5460](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/5460), [UniProt Q01860](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q01860/entry)). Somatic alterations involving POU5F1 have been reported in myoepithelial carcinoma of soft tissue/bone. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## PPARG

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

PPARG encodes peroxisome proliferator-activated receptor gamma. It participates in negative regulation of DNA-templated transcription and negative regulation of miRNA transcription. Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA-binding transcription factor activity and DNA-binding transcription activator activity, RNA polymerase II-specific. ([NCBI Gene 5468](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/5468), [UniProt P37231](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P37231/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with lipodystrophy (semidominant); PPARG-related familial partial lipodystrophy (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving PPARG have been reported in follicular thyroid carcinoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

## PPM1D

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

PPM1D encodes protein phosphatase 1D. It participates in regulation of intracellular signal transduction and regulation of transcription initiation by RNA polymerase II. Functional annotations also associate the protein with protein serine/threonine kinase activity and protein serine/threonine phosphatase activity. ([NCBI Gene 8493](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/8493), [UniProt O15297](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/O15297/entry)). Germline disease associations include syndromic intellectual disability (autosomal dominant); intellectual developmental disorder with gastrointestinal difficulties and high pain threshold (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving PPM1D have been reported in diffuse midline glioma, H3 K27-altered, lung adenocarcinoma, and basal cell carcinoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## PTCH1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

PTCH1 encodes protein patched homolog 1. It participates in signal transduction and positive regulation of DNA-templated transcription. ## PTEN

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

PTEN encodes a tumor suppressor that removes a phosphate from phosphatidylinositol-3,4,5-trisphosphate, reducing the cellular abundance of this signaling lipid. This activity opposes AKT signaling. Although PTEN contains a domain related to protein tyrosine phosphatases, it preferentially acts on phosphoinositide substrates. Its lipid-phosphatase activity therefore provides a direct biochemical mechanism for restraining a pathway that promotes cellular growth and survival. ([NCBI Gene 5728](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/5728)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with PTEN hamartoma tumor syndrome (autosomal dominant); Cowden syndrome 1 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving PTEN have been reported in endometrioid carcinoma of endometrium, prostate adenocarcinoma, and prostate. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [PMID 22610119](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22610119/), [PMID 29610475](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29610475/)).

## RB1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

RB1 encodes a tumor suppressor that negatively regulates cell-cycle progression and also helps maintain chromatin structure. In its active, hypophosphorylated state, the protein binds the E2F1 transcription factor. This interaction connects the protein's phosphorylation state with transcriptional control of the cell cycle. Its additional role in stabilizing heterochromatin links regulation of cell division with organization of the genome. ([NCBI Gene 5925](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/5925)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with retinoblastoma (autosomal dominant); hereditary retinoblastoma (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 25928201](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25928201/)). Somatic alterations involving RB1 have been reported in retinoblastoma, small cell lung carcinoma, and small cell lung cancer. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [PMID 26168399](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26168399/), [PMID 22941188](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22941188/)).

Pathogenic germline variants are associated with Jawad syndrome (autosomal recessive). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 21998596](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21998596/)).

Curation note (outside report text): somatic_association_requires_curation.

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

TEAD1 encodes transcriptional enhancer factor TEF-1. It participates in positive regulation of DNA-templated transcription and positive regulation of miRNA transcription. Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA-binding transcription factor activity and DNA-binding transcription activator activity, RNA polymerase II-specific. ([NCBI Gene 7003](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7003), [UniProt P28347](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P28347/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with helicoid peripapillary chorioretinal degeneration (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)).

Curation note (outside report text): somatic_association_requires_curation.

## TEK

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

TEK encodes angiopoietin-1 receptor. It participates in positive regulation of intracellular signal transduction and positive regulation of Rac protein signal transduction. Functional annotations also associate the protein with protein kinase activity and protein tyrosine kinase activity. ([NCBI Gene 7010](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7010), [UniProt Q02763](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q02763/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with TEK-related primary glaucoma (autosomal dominant); multiple cutaneous and mucosal venous malformations (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)).

Curation note (outside report text): somatic_association_requires_curation.

## TENT5C

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

TENT5C encodes terminal nucleotidyltransferase 5C polymerase FAM46C). It participates in mRNA stabilization and negative regulation of cell differentiation. Functional annotations also associate the protein with poly(A) RNA polymerase activity. ([NCBI Gene 54855](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/54855), [UniProt Q5VWP2](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q5VWP2/entry)). Somatic alterations involving TENT5C have been reported in plasma cell myeloma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip), [PMID 24434212](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24434212/)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## TENT5D

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

TENT5D encodes terminal nucleotidyltransferase 5D. Its functions include poly(A) RNA polymerase activity and mRNA stabilization. ([NCBI Gene 169966](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/169966), [UniProt Q8NEK8](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q8NEK8/entry)). Somatic alterations involving TENT5D have been reported in esophageal carcinoma and gastric adenocarcinoma. ([PMID 26619011](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26619011/)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## TERC

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

TERC encodes the RNA component of telomerase, providing the template used by the TERT catalytic subunit to add telomeric repeats to chromosome ends. ([NCBI Gene 7012](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7012)). Pathogenic constitutional TERC variants cause autosomal dominant dyskeratosis congenita, a telomere biology disorder associated with bone-marrow failure. ([PMID 11574891](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11574891/), [ClinGen SGC-107615.2](https://search.clinicalgenome.org/kb/gene-validity/CGGV:assertion_cfdf86d0-9441-40bf-a1da-ab2c85f775cd-2024-11-25T17:00:00.000Z)). Somatic copy-number gain involving the TERC region has been associated with progression of cervical neoplasia to invasive carcinoma. Regional gain is distinct from the constitutional loss-of-function mechanisms underlying telomere biology disorders. ([PMID 17054308](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17054308/)).

## TERT

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

TERT encodes the catalytic reverse-transcriptase component of telomerase. Together with the TERC RNA template, it adds telomeric repeats to chromosome ends. ([NCBI Gene 7015](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7015)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with dyskeratosis congenita, autosomal dominant 2 (semidominant); dyskeratosis congenita, autosomal dominant 2 (autosomal recessive). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving TERT have been reported in liposarcoma, paraganglioma, and chondrosarcoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

## TET1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

TET1 encodes methylcytosine dioxygenase TET1. It participates in negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II and positive regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA 5-methylcytosine dioxygenase activity and methyl-CpG binding. ([NCBI Gene 80312](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/80312), [UniProt Q8NFU7](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q8NFU7/entry)). Somatic alterations involving TET1 have been reported in colon adenocarcinoma, mixed germ cell tumor, and stomach adenocarcinoma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## TET2

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

TET2 encodes a methylcytosine dioxygenase that converts methylcytosine in DNA to 5-hydroxymethylcytosine. This enzymatic activity changes the chemical state of methylated DNA and is part of the regulation of epigenetic information. TET2 also participates in myelopoiesis, connecting its role in DNA modification with normal production and development of myeloid blood cells. Alternative transcripts encode different protein isoforms. ([NCBI Gene 54790](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/54790)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with immunodeficiency 75 (autosomal recessive). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving TET2 have been reported in myelodysplastic neoplasm, chronic myelomonocytic leukemia, and acute myeloid leukemia. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## TFE3

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

TFE3 encodes transcription factor E3. It participates in positive regulation of DNA-templated transcription and regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA-binding transcription factor activity and DNA-binding transcription activator activity, RNA polymerase II-specific. ## TP53

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

TP53 encodes p53, a tumor-suppressor transcription factor with DNA-binding, transcriptional-activation, and oligomerization domains. In response to cellular stress, p53 changes expression of target genes that control cell-cycle arrest, DNA repair, senescence, apoptosis, and metabolism. Alternative promoters, RNA splicing, and translation initiation produce multiple protein isoforms. These properties allow the gene to connect detection of cellular stress with several distinct growth-control and cell-fate responses. ([NCBI Gene 7157](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7157)). Germline pathogenic variants cause autosomal dominant Li-Fraumeni syndrome, which predisposes to a broad spectrum of cancers, notably early-onset breast cancer, sarcomas, central nervous system tumors, and adrenocortical carcinoma. ([GeneReviews: Li-Fraumeni Syndrome](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1311/), [PMID 8118819](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/8118819/), [PMID 1978757](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1978757/)). Somatic TP53 mutations occur across many cancer types. ([NCBI Gene 7157](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7157), [GeneReviews: Li-Fraumeni Syndrome](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK1311/)).

([NCBI Gene 7158](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7158), [UniProt Q12888](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q12888/entry)).

## TP63

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

TP63 encodes tumor protein 63. It participates in negative regulation of DNA-templated transcription and positive regulation of DNA-templated transcription. Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA-binding transcription factor activity and DNA-binding transcription factor activity, RNA polymerase II-specific. UROD encodes uroporphyrinogen decarboxylase. It participates in heme biosynthetic process and heme B biosynthetic process. Functional annotations also associate the protein with uroporphyrinogen decarboxylase activity. ([NCBI Gene 7389](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7389), [UniProt P06132](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P06132/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with UROD-related phototoxic porphyria (semidominant); familial porphyria cutanea tarda (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 19419417](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19419417/)).

Curation note (outside report text): somatic_association_requires_curation.

## USP28

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

USP28 encodes ubiquitin carboxyl-terminal hydrolase 28. It participates in protein deubiquitination and DNA damage response. Functional annotations also associate the protein with deubiquitinase activity and cysteine-type deubiquitinase activity. ([NCBI Gene 57646](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/57646), [UniProt Q96RU2](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q96RU2/entry)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation, somatic_association_requires_curation.

## USP6

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

USP6 encodes ubiquitin carboxyl-terminal hydrolase 6. It participates in protein deubiquitination and protein modification process. Functional annotations also associate the protein with cysteine-type deubiquitinase activity and cysteine-type endopeptidase activity. ([NCBI Gene 9098](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/9098), [UniProt P35125](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P35125/entry)). USP6 rearrangements can produce promoter-driven overexpression in primary aneurysmal bone cysts. ([PMID 15509545](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15509545/)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## USP8

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

USP8 encodes ubiquitin carboxyl-terminal hydrolase 8. It participates in Ras protein signal transduction and endosome organization. Functional annotations also associate the protein with K48-linked deubiquitinase activity and K63-linked deubiquitinase activity. ([NCBI Gene 9101](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/9101), [UniProt P40818](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P40818/entry)). Somatic alterations involving USP8 have been reported in hepatocellular carcinoma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## VAV1

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

VAV1 encodes proto-oncogene vav. It participates in small GTPase-mediated signal transduction and regulation of small GTPase mediated signal transduction. Functional annotations also associate the protein with guanyl-nucleotide exchange factor activity and phosphorylation-dependent protein binding. ([NCBI Gene 7409](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7409), [UniProt P15498](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P15498/entry)). Somatic alterations involving VAV1 have been reported in adult t-cell leukemia/lymphoma, peripheral t-cell lymphoma, NOS, and lung adenocarcinoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## VCL

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

VCL encodes vinculin. It participates in cell adhesion and cell-matrix adhesion. Functional annotations also associate the protein with molecular adaptor activity and structural molecule activity. Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

VGLL2 encodes transcription cofactor vestigial-like protein 2. It participates in regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## VHL

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

VHL encodes a component of a ubiquitination complex that targets hypoxia-inducible factor for degradation. Hypoxia-inducible factor is a transcription factor central to regulation of gene expression in response to oxygen, so VHL links protein turnover with oxygen-responsive signaling. The VHL protein also participates in processes involving cilia, cytokine signaling, cellular senescence, and formation of the extracellular matrix. ([NCBI Gene 7428](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7428)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with von Hippel-Lindau disease (autosomal dominant); Chuvash polycythemia (autosomal recessive). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving VHL have been reported in clear cell renal cell carcinoma, renal clear cell carcinoma, and pheochromocytoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## VIM

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

VIM encodes vimentin. It participates in intermediate filament organization and regulation of mRNA stability. Functional annotations also associate the protein with molecular adaptor activity and structural constituent of cytoskeleton. ([NCBI Gene 7431](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7431), [UniProt P08670](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P08670/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with cataract 30 (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download), [PMID 26694549](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26694549/)). Somatic alterations involving VIM have been reported in YAP1::KMT2A-rearranged sarcoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

## ZEB2

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ZEB2 encodes zinc finger E-box-binding homeobox 2. It participates in regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II and negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA-binding transcription factor activity, RNA polymerase II-specific and DNA-binding transcription repressor activity, RNA polymerase II-specific. ([NCBI Gene 9839](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/9839), [UniProt O60315](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/O60315/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with Mowat-Wilson syndrome (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)).

Curation note (outside report text): somatic_association_requires_curation.

## ZFHX3

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ZFHX3 encodes zinc finger homeobox protein 3. It participates in regulation of DNA-templated transcription and positive regulation of DNA-templated transcription. Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA-binding transcription factor activity and DNA-binding transcription factor activity, RNA polymerase II-specific. ([NCBI Gene 463](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/463), [UniProt Q15911](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q15911/entry)). Somatic alterations involving ZFHX3 have been reported in vulva/Vagina, plasma cell myeloma, and endometrial carcinoma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## ZFHX4

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ZFHX4 encodes zinc finger homeobox protein 4. It participates in regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA-binding transcription factor activity, RNA polymerase II-specific. ([NCBI Gene 79776](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/79776), [UniProt Q86UP3](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q86UP3/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with syndromic complex neurodevelopmental disorder (autosomal dominant); neurodevelopmental disorder (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)).

Curation note (outside report text): somatic_association_requires_curation.

## ZFTA

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ZFTA encodes zinc finger translocation-associated protein. It participates in negative regulation of DNA-templated transcription. ([NCBI Gene 65998](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/65998), [UniProt C9JLR9](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/C9JLR9/entry)). Somatic alterations involving ZFTA have been reported in ependymoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## ZHX3

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ZHX3 encodes zinc fingers and homeoboxes 3. Its functions include DNA-binding transcription factor activity and DNA-binding transcription factor activity, RNA polymerase II-specific. ([NCBI Gene 23051](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/23051), [UniProt Q9H4I2](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q9H4I2/entry)). Somatic alterations involving ZHX3 have been reported in colorectal carcinoma, gastric adenocarcinoma, and bladder urothelial carcinoma. ([PMID 26619011](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26619011/)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## ZMYM2

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ZMYM2 encodes zinc finger MYM-type protein 2. It participates in negative regulation of DNA-templated transcription. Functional annotations also associate the protein with ubiquitin conjugating enzyme binding. ([NCBI Gene 7750](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/7750), [UniProt Q9UBW7](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q9UBW7/entry)). Pathogenic germline variants are associated with syndromic complex neurodevelopmental disorder (autosomal dominant); neurodevelopmental-craniofacial syndrome with variable renal and cardiac abnormalities (autosomal dominant). ([GenCC snapshot](https://thegencc.org/download)). Somatic alterations involving ZMYM2 have been reported in myeloid/lymphoid neoplasm with tyrosine kinase fusion and prostate adenocarcinoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## ZMYM3

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ZMYM3 encodes zinc finger MYM-type protein 3. Its functions include zinc ion binding. Somatic alterations involving ZMYM3 have been reported in thymoma, prostate, and medulloblastoma. ([IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

## ZNF292

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ZNF292 encodes zinc finger protein 292. It participates in regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Functional annotations also associate the protein with DNA-binding transcription activator activity, RNA polymerase II-specific and DNA-binding transcription factor activity, RNA polymerase II-specific. ## ZNF384

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ZNF384 encodes zinc finger protein 384. It participates in regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II. Functional annotations also associate the protein with transcription cis-regulatory region binding and sequence-specific double-stranded DNA binding. ([NCBI Gene 171017](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/171017), [UniProt Q8TF68](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q8TF68/entry)). ZNF516 encodes zinc finger protein 516. Its functions include cis-regulatory region sequence-specific DNA binding and DNA-binding transcription factor activity, RNA polymerase II-specific. ## ZNRF3

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ZNRF3 encodes E3 ubiquitin-protein ligase ZNRF3. It participates in limb development and protein ubiquitination. Functional annotations also associate the protein with ubiquitin-protein transferase activity and ubiquitin protein ligase activity. ([NCBI Gene 84133](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/84133), [UniProt Q9ULT6](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q9ULT6/entry)). Somatic alterations involving ZNRF3 have been reported in adrenocortical carcinoma. ([Tumor Driver Atlas](https://mikexiong2.github.io/molecular-tumor-drivers/), [IntOGen 20240920](https://www.intogen.org/download?file=IntOGen-Drivers-20240920.zip)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.

## ZRSR2

Pathologist review needed — LLM-generated summary/review.

ZRSR2 encodes a splicing factor important for recognition and processing of introns, particularly those handled by the minor spliceosome. ([NCBI Gene 8233](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/gene/8233)). Somatic ZRSR2 mutations occur in myelodysplastic neoplasms. ([PMID 22389253](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22389253/)).

Curation note (outside report text): germline_association_requires_curation.
